प्रीमती ए एल . विजयकर को अंशकालिक आधार पर राष्ट्रीय उपनामा

का . मा . 1964 : - केन्द्रीय सरकार ने , होयसा धारक क्षेत्र ( जैन और विकास ) अधिनियम , 1957 (1987 का 20 ) 
को धारा 7 की उप-धारा ( 1 ) के अधीन जारी की गई और भारत के राजपत्र , भाग 2, खंड 3, उप - खंड ( ii ) के पृष्ठ 3578 
से 3582 पर प्रकाशित भारत सरकार के कोयला मंत्रालय की अधिसूचना का . पा . स 2325 तारीख 29 अगस्त, 1991 द्वारा 
उभ अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में गित भूमि फा अजेन फरने के अपने प्राशय की सूचना दी थी ; 


और केन्द्रीय सरकार को जानकारी में यह बास लाई गई है कि राजपन्न में उक्त मधिसूचना के प्रकाशन मे मुद्रण प्रकृति 
फी कुछ त्रुटियां पाई गई हैं ; 


अतः, अब, केन्द्रीय सरकार, उक्त अधिसुचना की धारा 7 को उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों और इस निमित अन्य 
सामर्यकारी शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए उन अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची में निम्नलिखिन संशोधन करती है : --

का आ 1965 - - केन्द्रीय सरकार , सरकारी स्थान 
( अप्राधिकृत अधिभोगियो को बेदखली ) अधिनियम , 1971 
( 1971 का 40 ) की धारा 3 द्वारा प्रदस शक्तियो का 
प्रयोग करते हा और खान मत्रालय की अधिसूचना का आ 
म 1584 तारीख 5 जून , 1989 को उन बातो के मिनाए 
अधिकात करते हुए जिन्हें ऐसे अधिक्रमण मे पहले किया गया 
है या करने का लोप किया गया है , नीचे सारणी के स्तम्भ 
( 1 ) में वर्णित अधिकारी को , जो मरकार के गजपत्रित 
अधिकारी की समतुल्य पक्ति का अधिकारी है, उक्त अधि 
नियम के प्रयोजनो के लिए संपदा अधिकारी नियुक्त करती है 
और आग निदेश देती है कि उक्त अधिकारी उक्त सारणी के 
स्तम्भ ( 2 ) की तत्स्थानी प्रविष्टि में विनिर्दिष्ट सरकारी 
स्थानी की बाबत अपनी अधिकारिता की स्थानीय सीमाओ 
के भीतर उक्त अधिनियम द्वारा या उसके अधीन मपदा 

नारी को प्रदत्त शषितया का प्रयोग और उस पर 
अधिरोपित कर्तव्यों का पालन करगा ।

का प्रा 1971 --- - चलचित्र प्रमाणन नियम , 1983 के नियमों , 
और 8 के माय पटिन चलचिब अधिनियम 1952 ( 1952 का 37 ) 
के बंड 5 के उपखंड ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हए 

और इस मंत्रालय के दिनांक 30 सितम्बर, 1991 की प्रधिमूलना सख्या 
809/ 2 / 91 - एफ ( सी ) के अनुक्रम में केन्द्र सरकार निम्ननिखिप्त व्यक्तियों 
को केन्द्रीय फिल्म प्रमाणन बोर्ड के कलकत्ता पैनेल के सदस्यो के हप में 
सत्काल प्रभाव से अगले प्राधेश होने तक नियुक्त करती है । 


दिल्ली विकास प्राधिकरण 

( मार्वजनिक सूचना ) 

नई दिल्ली, 14 जुलाई , 1992

workman , have no substance .

legally or beyond any reasonable doubt; as neither the Union

11 . Mr. Murthy of course , in support of his submissions

BETWEEN 
The Director , Government of India Production Centres , 

Ettumanoor, Kottayam Distr ct, Kerala . 
( By Sri A . A . Abdul Hassan , Adl. Central Government 
Standing Counsel , Ernakulan ) . 

AND 
The General Secretary , Central Government Small Scale 

Industries Organisation Empluyees Union , Ettuma 

noor, Kottayam District. 
( By Sri R . Rajasekharan Pillai, Advocate , Ernakulam ) 

SUPPLEMENTARY AWARD 
As per Order No. L - 42011 /81 - D . II ( B ) dated 5 - 3 - 1987 the 
Government of India have referied this Industrial dispute 
for adjudicat on to this lubrinal. 

2 . Altogether five issues have been referred for adjudica 
tion . After considering the con entions advanced by both 
sides and evidence adduced before this Tribural, by arvard 
dated 12 12 . 1988 this Tribunal allowed issue nunibers 2 to 4 
in favour of the workmen and nega ived the claim under 
ssue No. 1 . Both sides challeaged the award passed by this 
Tribunal before Central Administrative Tiibunal Ernakulam 
(CAT for short ) as per OA No . 403 /89 and OA No . 94 / 90 . 
The CAT as per decision dated 31. 8 1990 quashed the award 
of this Tribunal so far as it relates to issue No. I and a 
portion of issue No. III with regard to claim of uniform 
and directed th s Tribunal to consider these claims a fresh . 
That is how this matter is now before this Trihunal. 
3 . I shall summarise belo :v issues numbers I and III. 
I. “ Whether the Director of the Government of India 

Production Centres, Ettunanooi was justified in 
fixing 36 Helper Mistries recruited n 1971 - 72 as 
mistr es at minimum of the scales, 110 - 155 after 
one year of their service 17 t je lower scale , or 
whether they should have been given a l gher start 
in the scale as per their appoinıment orders ? If so , 

what should be pas 
III. "Whether the demand of Helpers unler the Director 

Government of India Production Centre , Ettuma 
noor for quasi- permanency , confirmation , provision 
of un forms, gratuity , pension and other service 
benefits applicable to regular employees is legal 
and justified ? If so , to wiat extent and subject to 
what conditions. " 


4 . The post of mistries and helpers ire two un ts of the 
cadre and Director of Produc ion Centre was the appointing 
authority . It is not dispute that the appointment was in a 
non - gazetted establishment. It is also not lisputed that as 
per memorandum No. 25 SSI ( C ) ( 13 ) !62 dated 18 - 9 - 1962 in 
Ext. M10 Award of Industrial Tribunal Madras and M8 
judgment of the H gh Court of Kerala and ir. the argument 
notes filed this Tričunal on behalf of management, the Presi 
dent of India delegated th : powers to make aprointments to 
Class IV post and Class III p .ist where the scale of pay does 
not exceed Rs. 300 - PM The Director of Production Centre 
as empowered as stated above offered arpointment to the 
candidates who were called for interview as is evident from 
Ext. W2. The scale of nay of Rs. 75 - 95 is clearly stated . 
It was also offered to une :1 higher scale of Rs 110 - 155 after 
seeing the performance of few months. Ext W2 also iefers 
the terms and cond tio ‘is of appointment . This offer Wos 
accepted by all the workers wild are now before this 
Tribunal. Ext M1 is one of the undertaking of accep ance 
submitted by Sri . K . K . Madhavan Nair. All the 36 helper 
mistries accepted the order of appointment and reported for 
duty . It is true that the vorkers were called for interview 
for the post of mistry in the pay scale of Rs. 110 - 155 as is evi 
dent from Ext . W1. But * ae offer of anno ntment was to the 
post of helper mistry in the pay scale of Rs. 75 - 95 which 
was accepted by the 36 persons and reported for city ard 
continued there without raisirg any objec ion . Since there 
was an offer and acceptance and also that there was no 
legal defect in the order of appointment, it is not upon 
the workmen to contend that the Director had no power 
to make such appointment. If the Director had no power to 
a point helper mistries as conterded by the union , the result 
viould he that the workers were not validly appointed to any 
rost . The Director in exercise of the power delegated to him 
for appointment of Class II and Class IV post appointed 
he per mistries and watched their performance and then 
appointed them as mistres. It was specifically stated in the 
order of appointment that h eher start wouli be given and 
after one year all 36 workinen were given the pay scale of 
Rs. 110 - 155 . It may be recalled that on behalf of 34 inistries 
their association had adjudicited the same matter before the 
Industrial Tribunal Madras in I .D 3174 and ID . 27174 and 
that Tribunal rejected the claim of the workers . That find 
ing was confirmed by the High Court of Kerala in OP -4228 
75 and writ appeal No . 83 77 . 


Issue No . 1. The case of i le union is that 36 permanent 
posts of mistries in the scale of Rs. 110 -155 was lving vacant 
at Government of India Production Centre , Ettumanoor 
( Production Centre for Short ) which was created by Gov 
ernment of India . The Director of Production Centre ( the 
Director for short ) requested the Employment Exchange 
specifying the post , qualification etc . and the exchange spon 
sored various candidates and 35 persons were selected as 
mistries (Class-III ) , Group C post . But offered a lower post 
of helper mistry ( Class - lv ) Group D in the scale of 
Rs. 75 - 95 . Further case is tilat there was an undertaking to 
give a higher start in the s: ale of Rs. 110 - 155 after seeing 
their performance for a few mortis. According to the union 
the appointment orders issued are not in accordance with 
the instructions of the Government of India These persons 
continued for one year ard they wire appointed in the scale 
of 110 - 155 . The case of the union is that the D rector is 
not competent to create a post, 1x - designation and de - grading 
of these workers as helper mistry (Class - IV ) is unjust and 


5 . The appointment of helper mistries is an appointment 
of Class IV post in the scale of pay below Rs. 300 P .M . 
and these are posts in a lower cadre than the mistries in 
Class III post. Both hese cadros are in the establishment. 
Therefore there cannot be any violation of delegation of 
financial powers. The post of he sers is in existence and 
therefore the appointment of he rer mistries does not amount 
to creation of a post. The rersons were selected as hel 
pers for apnointing them in the post of mistries in future 
and they were so appointed alco But it does not amount to 
redesignation or de- grading of he post . There is no altera 
tion in the condition of recruitinent or service . There was 
no lowering of the quil fication also . So there s no vioin inn 
of O . M . No F28 ( 1 ) EF1 88 dated 12 - 7 - 1958 . These persons 
were not fixed in a lower scale but offered the helpers post 


3190 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 25 , 1992 / SRAVANA 3 , 1914 


[PART II - Sec . 3 ( 1) ] 


www 


- 


with undertaking to give higher start watching their perfor 
mance . Subsequently they were g ven higher start and the 
post of mistries. So the question of lowering the scale of pay 
does not arise . 


6 . The terms and conditions of appointment were also not 
changed . After intervicw these workers were kept under senior 
mistries for one year is under study and those who were 
found satisfactory were regularised as spoken to by MW2. 
MW2 has further deposed that these workers were doing 
operations of machines and by putting them as helper nis 
tries it was intended to enable thron to attain the capacity 
to handle the machines independerly . After watching their 
performance for one year they were regularised as mistries. 
This arrangement was worked out exercising the acimin 
istrative discretion availab e to the appointing authority under 
the existing rules . Therefore the question ot violating Lm 
ployment Exchange Act, 1959 or any other rules does not 
also arise . Now the contention that in Exts . W3 and W4 
these persons are described as mistries and therefore that 
proves that these persons are working as inistries from the 
date of appointment. But that was clarificd by MW2 who 
issued Exts. W3 and W4 that it wits a mistake and the pur 
pose of their appointment was to learn job of mistries . 
Therefore Exts. W3 and W4 does nut prove that these 
workers were working is to stries from the date of appoint. 
ment. 

7 . From the above discussion it is clear that there is no 
merit in the contentions advanced by the union under issue 
No. I There is no illegality , injustice or violation of the 
principles of natural justice in the process of selection and 
appointment of these workmen as mistries . These workmen 
were appointed by their writton consent only . Hay ng ac 
cepted the offer of appointment without any objection what 
so -ever and joined duty and continued there , these workers 
cannot now turn round and contend that process of selec 
tion was illegal and the management has violated rules and 
circulars . There are no justifiable reasons to hold that the 
Director Production Centre has violated any rules or direc 
tions of the Government of India . The result is that the 
workers are not entitled to any relief as claimed under 
issue No . I. 

8 . The next claim of the union is regarding uniform and 
washing allowance which is included under issue No. III . 
According to the union the persons working n industrial 
establishment are provided with two sets of uniform in an 
year and are granted with washing allowance . Since tho 
workers in this case are appointed in Class IV category , they 
are entitled to get two sets of uniform and washing allow 
ance according to the union as per Annexure - III to Gov 
ornment of India , Ministry of Home Affairs , OM No. 5 4158 
Public - II dated 27 - 2 - 1962 . It is opposed by the managenent 
mainly on the ground that these workmen are not eligible 
for uniform as per " Ilan BUOK of Uniform of Gr. C and 
D Employees" since issue of un forms to Government of 
India employees is regulated by this book issued by Gov 
ernment of India . According to the management skillent 
workers and helpers are eligible for Aprons as per Ext. M3 
letter dated 6 - 6 - 1985 . The clajm of uniform has already been 
considered and rejected by the Industrial Tribunal. Madras 
in I . D . 374 and 27 74 as s cvident from Ext. M10 award . 
The learned counsel for the union argued that these workers 
are entitled to get uniform as per Annexure II to Govern 
ment of India circular dated 27- 2 - 1972 mentioned above . 
But it is evident from pages 52 :ud 53 of Hand Book of 
uniforms mentioned above the workers in question are not 
included in the category of employees who are elig ble for 
uniforms in Class III and Chiess IV employees. Further, as 
per Ext. M3 order they are eligible to get Aprons . No evi 
dence is forthcoming in support of the claim of urifoim . 
WW1 has stated that the workers are eligjhle for uniform 
and that they are entitled to get un form allowance . It is 
also stated that workers handling machines and chemicals 
are eligible for uniforms as jer rules. But no such miles as 
stated in the claim statement were brought to the notice of 
this Tribunal. There is also no evidence to show that similar 
category of workers in other establ shments are getting uni 
forms. WW1 has not spoken a word about any establish 
ment supplying uniform to similarly placed emplovees . 
MW2 has categorically denied the claim of the workmen 
which is supported by the Hand Book of uniforms and Ext. 
M3 order. Since the anion failed to establish the elig bility 
of the workers for uniform and washing allowance under 


any statute or rules as againsi the documentary evidence of 
management mentioned above , the present claim is only to 
be rejected . I do so . 

9 . In view of the above discussion , I hold that the claim 
of the workers under issue No . I and the clam of uniform 
and washing allowance under issue No . III are devoid of 
merit and unsustainable . The workers are therefore not en 
titied to any relief . 
An award is passed accordingly . 

C . N . SASIDHARAN , Industrial Tritunal

coquiry . By the same letter the managemcat ordered a 
domcstic cuquiry and appointed Shri Mohit Mukherjee ,

Officer to conduct an enquiry into the charges levelled against 
him . The Enquiry Officer held the enquiry after due notice 
to the concerned workman who participated in the enquiry . 
All thh documents relied on by the management were pro 
duced in the enquiry and the same were shown to him and 
he put his signature thereon . The managemont examined 
Shri R . Mohan as first witness and the concerned workman 
cross examined him partly with the assistance of his co -worker 
Bct thereafter he did not turn up for completion of cross-exa

hlm . Smeeth did not turn up in the enquiry , the next witness of 
the management, K . Prasad was examined ex -parte in his 
abscoce . Theroafter the Enquiry Officer submitted his report 
of enugiry dated 25-4 - 87. In the report the Enquiry Officer

produced by the managemcot in the coquiry the charges 
framed against the concerned workman were fully establ shed . 
Thereafter tho matter was considered by the Manager , Nooni 
dih - Jitpur Colllery who accepted the findings of the E 
Officer and came to the conclusion that this was a fit case 
for dismissal of the concerned workman from service . 
Thercafter the matter was also considered by the Superinten 
dent (J), Noonidih - Jitpur Colļicry ; he also agreed with the

filo was subsequently put up before the Chief Mining

workman should be dismissed from service . Accordingly , he

Jitpur Colliery . In the circumstances , the management has

From the evidence of these two witnesses it appears that 
niechan scd attendunce shecis used to be prepared on the 
basis of attendance registers and other registers and these 
mechanised attendance sheets used to be sent to D . P . C . 
at Purppur for billing Olwages . It is the case of the 
management that the concerned workman used 10 post 
intlated figures in respect cf somo daily rated workmen in 
the mechaniscd attendance sheet and D . P . C . used 10 bill 
Wage - slicets on the basis of such mechanised attendance 
sheet and in the process excess payments were made to 
these workmen and the concerned Workman adopted tho 
subterfuge of crasing out the inflated figures in inochanised 
attendance sheets and replaced the Same by autual figure . 
In order to prove this fact that tho D . P . C . billed wage 
Bheet as per mechanised attendance sheet , it was necessary 
for the management to examine witness from the D . P . C . 
in order to sustain the fact that the D . P . C . billed the 
amount as per mechanised attendance shect . It appears 
that one end of the rope was at Noonodih - Jitpur colliery 
and the other and at D . P . C . at Burnpur. The mechani.ed 
attendance was used to be prepared at Noonodih - Jitpur 
coll ery while billing of vage as per mechanised attendance 
sheet was done by D . P . C . at Burnpur . Hence, in my view , 
a compotent person from D . P . C . should have been exa 
mined by the management in domestic enquiry . 

Thon again , Shri Mohan claimed in the preliminary en 
quiry that he was in a position to identify the handwriting 
of Shri Şinha , thc concerned workman , in the mechanised 
attendance sheet for the period from April, 1983 to Murch - - 
May , 1984 . But he did nothing of the soit in the Jomiy 
tic enquiry . That being so , the allegation of crasing out of 
the inflated figuros by the concerned workman has not been 
established to the bill . 

Shri Mohan stated that the concerned workman inflated 
the figure in respect of 52 and 26 vaily ralıd employees 
with malafide intention and ulterior motive for his per onal 
gain . Tho Enquiry Officer also held that the conceined 
workmen willfully / deliberately with malafide intention for 
the purpose of personal gain booked inilated figures in the 
mechanised aţion lance 9hcet in le peut of 52 daily jated em 
ployees of Engineering Departinent during thọ year 1983 -84 
and by this fraud the concerned workman caused excess 
puym nt to the tune of Rs. 1 . 43 ,906 07 . The e is not 
4 whit of evidence that the concerned workman , by his 
allogod act of inflating figuro in mechanised attondance 
shçct he made any personnel pain or that he had an ul er 
lor ipotive in doing so , On the contrary , some of the work 
men concerned who ieceived exceng payment wric ex mined 
in the preliminary Cuquiry and they did not inculcate the 
concerned workman . The management has subsequently re 
alised the excess amount paid to these workmen . This being 
So , I find no objective hasis to hold that the concerned 
workman allegedly committed any act of misconduct by in 
dating tho figures of attendince of some of the daily rated 
workmen of Noonodih - Jitruir colliery from April , 1983 to 
April, 1984 for personal gain or with an ulterior motive .

should be disallowed .

7. The photo copies of the true copics of chargesheet

Admittedly there was a criminal case and certified copy of

Admittedly , the judgement of the Criminal Court cannot

management and the order passed thereunder . However , the 
learend counsel for the workmen contended that it has got 
its own use and value for collateral purpose . At this stage 
one authority has been cited on behalf of the management 

rted in 1985 Vol. 11 LLJ paga 364 wherein their l .ord 
ship held that domestic enquiry can be proceeded even after 
acquittal by the Criminal Court. This means the judgement

no bar for the departmental endquiry . Anyway , in the instant 
case we see that the domestic enquiry was held much carlier 
and the result of the criminal Court came after about 12 
years .

flent here before this Tribunal las been introduced as au 
after thought most probably to magnity tho severity of the 
Incident although little knowing about its adverse effect,

13 Be it as it may , the question is whether thc workman 
is entitled to the benelits of the ID Act Admittedly the 
workman had worked for 105 days which is much less than 
240 days of work in a calender year, therefore , in his case 
the provisions of sec . 25F , 25G and 25H of the ID . Act 
would not apply . For the application of section 25F ID 
Act, it is necessary that the workman must havc bech in 
continuous service for not less than one year undei any 
employor prior to the date of his termination / retrenchment 
Section 256 of the ID Act is to be read with Rules 77 
of the ID . (Central) Ruley and Sec 25H of the ID . Act is 
1o be read with Rule 78 Rules 77 and 78 apply to a woih 
man as celined in Rule 76 of the ID , (Central) Rulty and 
according to Rule 76 such a workman should be a person 
who had worked continuously for not less than one year 
prior to the termination of his services . Continuous one 
yeai service within the nicaning of Section 25 of ID Act , 
means working for 240 days atleast during a year There 
fore , in his case the provisions of Section 25G , 25H of the 
Act too are not applicable 

14 I may make it clear that it is a matter of common

ordinate cadre I herefore , individual manages are not

ment .

16 Therefore , from the above discussions of facts and 
law I find that the Union has failed to prove his case that 
the workman was appointed against a reglar vacancy of 
a peon ( Sepoy ) by the management and also that the bank

sides violations of Articles of the Constitution of India 

17 Hence it is held that the action of the management

titled to no relief
